उपधारा (8) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, श्री एस.वी.राजू, भारत के एडिशनल सॉलिसिटर जनरल, को विशेष 
न्यायाधीश के न्यायालय सहित विचारण न्यायालय और कर्नाटक राज्य के उच्च न्यायालय के समक्ष, दिल्‍ली विशेष पुलिस 
स्थापन (केंद्रीय अन्वेषण ब्यूरो) द्वारा संस्थित मामला संख्या आरसी 47(एस)/2049-सीबीआई/एसीबी/ बीएलआर 
(धारवाड हत्या मामला - विशेष मामला सं. 565/2 और 7856/2) के अभियोजन का और तत्समय प्रवृत्त विधि द्वारा 
स्थापित किसी अपील या पुनरीक्षण न्यायालय में, ऐसे मामलों से उद्भूत, किसी अपील, पुनरीक्षण या अन्य मामलों का 
संचालन करने हेतु, पदभार ग्रहण करने की तारीख से या मामले का निपटान होने तक या अगले आदेशों तक, इनमें से जो 
भी पूर्वतर हो, विशेष लोक अभियोजक के रूप में नियुक्त करती है।

आपदा प्रबंधन विभाग के माध्यम से जारी सम्मति से, श्री हिमांशु शेखर, मुख्य वाणिज्यिक निरीक्षक, दक्षिण पूर्व रेलवे, 
रांची मंडल, भारतीय रेलवे, रेल मंत्रालय के विरुद्ध भ्रष्टाचार निवारण afafaaa-7988 (20/8 में यथा संशोधित) की

का.आ. 4787.--औदघधोगिक विवाद अधिनियम 4947 (4947 का 44) की धारा 47 के अनुसरण में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार सेंट्रल बैंक ऑफ इंडिया के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजको और श्री गोविन्द अंकुश शिंदे के बीच 

अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में श्रम न्यायालय अहमदनगर के पंचाट (3/20I7) प्रकाशित करती है। 
[a. एल-39025/0/2026-आई आर (बी- ॥)-76]

( Date: 25-07-2024) 


lL, This Reference IDA Application filed by Second Party Govind Ankush Shinde against First Party Central 
Bank of India, branch Mandavgan District Ahmednagar where he was acting as Sweeper. However, on .4.207 he 
was removed from service on the basis of oral order. Being aggrieved by said order he has filed this said reference 
IDA application for reinstatement alongwith all benefits. Brief facts of the Application are as under- 


2. The second party Govind Ankush Shinde (Labour) is Sweeper towards the first party Central Bank of India ( 
Bank) at branch Mandavgan since 2008. Initially his salary was Rs. [00/- only. Neither any appointment letter, nor 
any salary slip given to him. He has given unblemished and continuous service since 2008 to 20I7 near about 9 
years. On |.4.20I7 he was dismissed from service by oral order. Therefore, he raised said issue before Labour 
Commissioner at Pune. However, there was no settlement and therefore such reference. He was many times 
requested for his reinstatement however Bank pay no heed. In between, in the year 203 there was process started for 
recruitment, he has also taken part in the said recruitment however no appointment was given to him. Further Bank 
did not comply the Section 25-F, GH. Actually he was given 240 days service and his post was permanent. Though 
recruitment process was held however no appointment was given to him. His last salary was Rs. 30/- per day. He 
was dismissed from services against principles of natural justice without any permission. 


3: On the contrary, other workers namely Keru Suryabhan Borude, Dikshant Ghogare, Vinod Ghaytadak, 
Abasaheb Kale was kept on establishment. The second party was removed from service without any valid reason 
and legal way. Therefore oral order dated |-4-207 needs to be vacated and it prayed that the second party should 
appoint alongwith salary and all benefits. Hence, this application. 


4. In pursuance of notice first party Nos. | & 2 Central Bank appeared and filed their detail written statement at 
Exh. C-6. 
5. In between the address of the bank was changed and necessary amendment was taken on record. A pursis has 


filed to read the original Say of Bank. Pursis was at Exh. C-22. 


6. Bank has filed detail Say at Exh C-6 and denied all the averments in application. According to the first party 
application is not legal and maintainable as per law. The second party has never locus-standi to file such application. 
The application was filed beyond prescribed period of limitation. It was specifically denied that the bank has given 
oral order on |.4.207 regarding dismissal of the said second party. The last salary of Rs. 30/- per day was denied 
by the bank. It is clarified by bank that no any appointment was given to the second party. Actually the appointment 
of the employee, dismissal, promotions these rights were not vested with the bank. It was vested towards Zonal 
officer. All appointments were fulfilled by after giving public notice in news paper. There was no any rule to give 
permanency to the workers who have completed 60 or 45 days. There was no any classification as temporary or 
permanent Sweeper in bank. The second party ought to appoint as per rules and conditions given to him in 
advertisement. No condition under sec. 25 F, G and H and rule 80, 8] were applicable to bank. It was also denied 
that, his service was unblemished. Actually he has filed matter towards Labour Officer without any valid reason. He 
never given continuous service to the bank, nor bank dismissal him. Actually his work was temporary and he has 
given per day salary. Whenever work was available bank used to call him, he never worked as permanent or 
temporary worker towards bank. Therefore no question arises regarding any notice pay or compensation. As he was 
not in service towards bank no question arise to prepare any seniority list, no any particular work allotted to him. Now 
a days there was no work available which was suitable to him. Already he was going towards another bank regarding 
his work. He has filed this application only to harass the bank. Lastly, they prayed for rejection of this application 
with cost. 


he On the basis of application and W:S. filed on record my learned predecessor has framed issues at Exh. O-6, 
to which I have recorded my findings thereon with the reasons mentioned below- 


Sr. No. ISSUES FINDINGS 


Does the second party prove that the first party has illegally and improperly Negative 
terminated his services by oral order dated |.4.207, violating the statutory 
provisions of Industrial Disputes Act, 947? 


2 Does the Second Party prove that, he is entitled for the relief of reinstatement Does not survive 
with continuity of service and full back wages w.e.f. .4.207 ? 


3 What Award As per final order

cutting of recruitment in Loksatta newspaper, application of second party dated 4-l-2024, news regarding cancellation 
of recruitment dated 3-[-2024 , letter from association dated l0..2024 and some receipts placed on record. 


9. To substantiate their claim, the second party examined himself at Exh. U-6 and reiterated the contention in 
the application. I have refrain myself to mention the same. He has filed evidence closed pursis at Exh. U-l6. Per 
contra, bank has examined one Shri Makarand Kasabe at Eh. C-l2 and one another witness namely Sonali Jadhav at 
Exh. C-6 and filed evidence closed pursis at Exh. C-2]. 


0. According to the second party, he has given unblemished and continuous service for 9 years and therefore, he 
has eligible as permanent employee of bank. However, bank did not given permanency to him. On the contrary, on 
.4.207 he has been dismissed from service. Actually there is some procedure ought to be adopted by bank regarding 
permanency. According to this second party the employees who has completed 60 days and now in 45 days these 
employees are eligible for permanency and therefore to prove the said fact burden shifts on the shoulder of the second 
party himself, but he did not succeed the same. He has to prove the some one has given permanency by adopting this 
procedure. According to him, on |.4.20!7 he removed from service and it was oral termination. I have minutely 
gone through with all documents filed on record. However no any sufficient evidence placed on record by the second 
party except some answers regarding vouchers. Learned Advocate Kakad turned my attention to cross examination. 
Actually Central Bank is Nationalized bank and such bank having no powers to dismiss to any one by oral 
termination. Furthermore, the recruitment process has to adopt by bank. Though the second party submitted that he 
has Sweeper since 28.2.2008 however no any documentary evidence placed on record. Per contra, he has admitted 
at Exh. U-6 that the appointment of any employee has to fill up only by due procedure by public advertisement in 
news paper. However, in the year 2008 no any advertisement had been published by bank. Also no any written or 
oral examination was held. There was no any agreement between him and bank regarding his service. Also no 
appointment letter given by bank. He has also admitted that bank has not given any particular work to him. Further 
Kakad Advocate turned my attention that he has no authority to sign any register of the bank. Also he has no any 
authority to record any entries in register and therefore, no any cogent evidence placed on record by the second party. 


ll. To substantiate their claim, he mentioned that on |.4.20l7 he was removed from service. His service was 
clean and unblemished. However no any appointment letter or salary slip given to him. Also compensation of sec. 
25-F, 25-G, and 25-H not given to him. Advocate Kakad has taken cross of the second party and turned my attention 
that in the year 2008 no any advertisement given by the bank and no any written and oral examination taken by bank. 
Neither any appointment letter, nor salary slip given to him. He has no authority to sign any register. No signature 
taken on attendance register. The voluminous documents placed on record regarding recruitment process in the year 
203 at Exh. U-27 but thereafter said recruitment process was cancelled. The copy of order in W.P. No. 379/ 2024 
and W.P. No. 459/ 205 order of Hon'ble Jabalpur High Court placed on record. However these matters are not 
connected with the matter in hand. The recruitment notification No. 2425 was also placed on record at Exh. U-33. 
Copy of order in W.P. No. 378॥/ 20i4 dated 4-3-20l6 of Hon'ble Apex Court placed on record. I have minutely 
gone through with all these documents. However, did not find any relevancy of these matters regarding matter in 
hand in my opinion, any recruitment process must be clean, transparent and definite no may one can allow to appoint 
above process. 


2. When any one make prayer of permanency he has to prove his entry was valid one and burden shifts on his 
shoulder. Learned Advocate Shri Modgekar submitted that no seniority list was published. He also mentioned some 
names like Keru Suryabhan Borude, Dikshant Ghogare, Vinod Ghaytadak, Abasaheb Kale etc. they were in service 
but such argument though attractive but not digestible. He has to make their own case on own footing. The judgment 
of Court were not depend on qualitative but depend on quantity therefore I do not wish to comment in case of

other workers. 


3. Learned Advocate Shri Modgekar turned my attention to the recruitment process in 20I3 and stay orders 
passed in W.P. No. 365 and 374/204. The advertisement was published in English and sweeper does not understood 
the same. It should be vernacular local language but thereafter whole process was cancelled. The reference should 
not be taken from said matter. At Exh. U-23 there are some letters regarding recruitment process of Sweepers. Also 
copy of orders of other Hon'ble High Courts, Guvahati and Nagpur bench. The copy of W.P. No. 356/ 206, W.P. No. 
8275/ 20l8 was placed on record. I have minutely gone through with orders of Hon'ble High Court in W.P. No. 8275/ 
208 Sandip Prahalat Engole & others. However, in this matter there was memorandum of settlement and if any 
recruitment was started than these sweepers has to be considered on priority basis. In case in hand no specific order 
given by any Hon'ble High Court. In W.P. No. [7273/ 20l6 Pradipkumar Shuklya Vs. Central Bank of India 
recruitment process was suspended. In short, there was many case filed by Sweepers against Central bank and facts 
are different. In my opinion, the second party has to prove their case by filing some probable evidence however in 
this matter no any documentary evidence given by the second party. Per contra, Central bank examined on Makarand 
Kasabe at Exh. C-2 and Sonali Jadhav at Exh. C-6. In cross examination very much reliance given on the 
vouchers. Actually these are witnesses of bank and in my opinion only vouchers of the documents could not decide 
merit in their prayer and therefore we could not rely on oral testimony of these bank officers. The signatures of the 
employee is to give on bank vouchers. Another employee Sunil Gatane was appointed said period. The date and time 
was mentioned on vouchers regarding payment. They tried to came on record except these evidence no other 
evidence available with second party. Shri Makarand Kasabe already deposed that he could not assign the salary of 
Shri Shinde. 


4. In cross examination of Sonali Jadhav very much relied on voucher. The expenses of daily wages worker 
used to shown in daily wages account. However, the reasons of the expenses not mentioned on voucher. No any 
documents available with bank regarding daily wages worker. In my opinion, for appointment of any employee there 
should be first creation of post and thereafter advertisement must be published in news paper. In present matter he 
was appointed in 2008 and having salary of Rs.l00/- per day and he has working 45 days did not prove by him and no 
any documents placed on record regarding the same. Though whole case depend upon other employee who were in 
service and vouchers available towards bank and therefore, I do not think that he was eligible for reinstatement in 
service only on some admissions given in cross examination. 


ID; Learned Advocate Kakad placed reliance on Secretary State of Karnataka and Ors. Vs. Umadevi ( AIR 
2006 SC 806 (SC) wherein it is held that- 


"Constitution of India] Art 6, Art 62, Art 226, Art 4, Art 39(d) Public employment Appointment dehors 
due process of selection envisaged by constitutional scheme- Confers no right on appointee- Regularisation of daily 
wager, temporary/ contractual employees appointed in violation of constitutional scheme- Right of Court/ executive 
would not extend to directing regularisation to be treated as permanence in service- Fact that employee has continued 
for long, irrespective- That apart, principle of equal pay for equal work cannot also be applied for giving relief of 
permanency- Doctrine of legitimate expectation cannot be invoked by such employees merely because in past State 
had regularised similarly placed on record. 


It has also to be clarified that merely because a temporary employee or a casual wage worker is continued for 
a time beyond the term of his appointment, he would not be entitled to be absorbed in regular service or made 
permanent, merely on the strength of such continuance, if the original appointment was not made by following a due 
process of selection as envisaged by the relevant rules. It is not open to the court to prevent regular recruitment at the 
instance of temporary employees whose period of employment has come to an end or of ad hoc employees who by the 
very nature of their appointment, do not acquire any right. High Courts acting under Article 226 of the Constitution of 
India, should not ordinarily issue directions for absorption, regularization, or permanent continuance unless the 
recruitment itself was made regularly and in terms of the constitutional scheme. Merely because, an employee had 
continued under cover of an order of Court, which we have described as ‘litigious employment in the earlier part of 
the judgment, he would not be entitled to any right to be absorbed or made permanent in the service. In fact, in such 
cases, the High Court may not be justified in issuing interim directions, since, after all, if ultimately the employee 
approaching it is found entitled to relief, it may be possible for it to mould the relief in such a manner that ultimately 
no prejudice will be caused to him, whereas an interim direction to continue his employment would hold up the 
regular procedure for selection or impose on the State the burden of paying an employee who is really not required. 
The courts must be careful in ensuring that they do not interfere unduly with the economic arrangement of its affairs 
by the State or its instrumentalities or lend themselves the instruments to facilitate the bypassing of the constitutional 
and statutory mandates. 


In my opinion in this case, criteria mentioned regarding promotion on temporary employee and daily wages. 
When there was any appointment in Nationalized bank there must be apply principle of equity. There should not be 
any violation of these principle of equity in between daily wages, contract labour and seasonal workers. "Same 
workers same pay" “leku dke leku osru* these principle is not apply here and therefore, others State apply these rules 
must be apply case in hand. These principle is not sustained. In case in hand Shri Modgekar submitted that same 


[भागवा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : अगस्त, 22, 2026/ श्रावण 3, 948 9459 


other workers are in service and such rule must be apply to case in hand however Rule of equality in public 
employment is basic feature of out Constitution and therefore this court having no right to give appointment of a 
particular employee and violate these fundamental principle. The same fact mentioned in case of Umadevi that the 
workers who are doing job on basis of honorarium should not be permanent. 


6. The Learned Advocate Shri Modgekar relied on Chief Conservator of Forests, Pune and another Vs. 
Janabai Sonaba Sarpale (209 (I) Bom.L.C. 8) wherein it is held that- 


"Maharashtra Recognition of Trade Unions and Prevention of Unfair Labour Practices Act, 7977, Section 
30, Sch-IV, Items 6,9- Regularisation- power of Industrial Court- Plea raised that in view of Uma Devi’ case- 
Industrial Court has no power to regularise- Held, once a case of unfair labour practice is established, Industrial 
Court has power to regularise services.” 


The case of Umadevi is not applicable here. However, in this above case the Court has declared unfair labour 
practice but case in hand facts are different one. 


7. The Advocate Shri Modgekar also relied upon the following judgments- 


l. H.D. SinghV Vs. Reserve Bank of India and others ( S.C. Labour Judgments 984 to 987 p 60), 
wherein it is held that- 


“Industrial Disputes Act, /947-5९९०॥०08 2(00), 25-B- “Retrenchment” meaning of- Dailyrated worker- His 
name struck off rolls on ground of passing matriculate examination — In instant case retrenchment of appellant by

2. Trade-Wings Limited Vs. Prabhakar Dattararam Phodkar of Bombay and Ors. (992 I CLR, 480) 
wherein it is held that- 


"§-25-F Industrial Disputes (Bombay) Rules, 7957-76 8I-Display of Seniority List Category-wise before 
effecting retrenchment-Failure to display such list-Effect- The said provision being mandatory, retrenchment will have 
to be quashed." 


Both the matters were relating to permanent employee. Therefore, ratio does not apply to case in hand. 


3. Umesh Saxena Vs. Presiding Officer, Labour Court, Agra and others ( 993 FLR (66) 566) wherein it is 
held that- 


"Section 25-F of the Act would certainly apply to the petitioner even if his appointment was adhoc or 
temporary. The fact that he had worked for more than 240 days in a year immediately preceding the issuance of 
termination order would make it imperative for the employer to follow the procedure laid down under the Act." 


It is not proved he has given 240 days service. 


4. Divisional Secretary, Maharashtra State Board of Secondary and Secondary Education, Nagpur and 
another Vs. Mohd. Naim S/o Abdul Rahim ( 2009 () Bom.LC 453 (Bom)) wherein it is held that- 


“Sections 25-F, 25-G, 25-H- Termination- And, reinstatement of an employee-Mode, or manner of entry into 
service- Is one of relevant factors, to be considered-And, distinction between cases of prayer for regularization and 
reinstatement being made earlier, is now not available after Constitutional Bench of Apex Court in the case of 
Secretary State of Karnataka Vs. Umadevi-In public employment.” 


5. Jayantibhai Raojibhai Patel Vs. Municipal Council, Narkhed ( Civil Appeal No. 688/ 209 ) wherein it 
is held that- 


"Illegal termination-Reinstatement without back wages- Denial of back wages to employee who has suffered 
due to illegal act of employer would amount to indirectly punishing employee concerned and rewarding employer by 
relieving him of obligation to pay back wages including emolument- Charge of misconduct not substantiated- 
Convening of fresh enquiry without recording any reasons.” 


No question arise regarding back wages. 
6. M.P. Electricity Board, Vidisha Vs. Hariram and another ( 2000 (57) FLR, 750) wherein it is held that- 


“ Section 25-F- Constitution of India] /959-4#0० 226-Award of reinstatement- passed by Industrial Court 
in appeal Employees engaged in daily wages." 


7. Samishta Dube Vs. City Board, Etawah & Anothr ( 999 LLR 460), wherein it is held that- 


"Section 6-P read with Section 6(N)- Retrenchment-Last come, first go- principle applicable even in case of 
those employed on daily wages- Section 6-P does not require any particular period of continuous service." 


No ratio apply to case in hand and there is recruitment process.

"25-F Workman-Definition of- Does not make any distinction between part time employee and full time

No question arise regarding back wages. 


0. Bright Export Limited Vs. Central Board of Trustees, EPF Organisation ( 206 LLR 487) wherein it is

“ When a particular fact is within the special knowledge of the opposite party, the burden t prove the same 
lies upon the opposite party in terms of section 796 of the Indian Evidence Act, 7672, even if the same has been 
pleaded by other party." 


ll. Sanjay Kumar S/o Surendra Kumar Sharma Vs. Chief Executive Officer, Janpad Panchayat, Ratlam ( 
200 LLR 065) wherein it is held that- 


"If the party possesses best evidence and is not produced before the Court, an adverse inference can be 
drawn under the Evidence Act against such a party." 


No appointment letter was given and salary given day to day therefore ratio not applicable to here.

Ratio not applicable here.

No. 379 of 2024 dated 2--2024) 


In the present matter the language of advertisement should be in vernacular as per verdict of Hon'ble

Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. CGIT.No. [/23/2003) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal- 
cum-Labour Court, Mumbai- |, as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management of M/s. 
Air India and their workmen.

JAIDEEP YADAV, Dy. Director

Mumbai dated 0™ June 2026. 
AWARD 


The present industrial dispute was referred by the Central Government vide Order No. L-l02/29/2003-IR(C-I) 
dated 4.05.2003 under Section !0()(d) and l0(2A) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 for adjudication of the 
following issues: 


I. Whether the contract between Air India and its contractors was sham and bogus and a 
camouflage to deprive the concerned workmen of benefits available to permanent 
workmen of Air India? 


2. Whether the concerned workmen should be declared permanent workmen of Air India? 
3. What wages and consequential benefits are payable to the concerned workmen? 


The dispute originated from Writ Petition No. 299] of 999 filed before the Hon’ble Bombay High Court wherein 
directions were issued to the Central Government to refer the industrial dispute for adjudication. Pursuant thereto, the 
present reference came to be registered before this Tribunal. 


During the pendency of the proceedings, the parties entered appearance and filed their respective pleadings, 
documents and evidence. Subsequently, the Claimants, represented through Kamgar Utkarsha Sabha and its General 
Secretary Shri Chintan Joshi, approached the Management for an amicable settlement of the dispute. 


A Memorandum of Settlement has thereafter been executed between Air India Limited and the 52 Claimants covered 
under the present Reference. The Settlement records that the parties, after several rounds of discussions, have 


9462 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : AUGUST 22, 2026/ SHRAVAN 3], 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


voluntarily resolved all disputes forming the subject matter of the reference. The settlement provides for payment of 
retrenchment compensation, gratuity and other monetary benefits as detailed in Annexure-A annexed thereto. The 
names of all 52 claimants and the amounts payable to each of them are specifically incorporated in the settlement. 


The Settlement further records that the concerned claimants have accepted the amounts mentioned against their 
respective names in full and final satisfaction of all claims arising out of the present reference and have agreed not to 
pursue any further claim of reinstatement, permanency, wages or consequential benefits against Air India Limited. It 
is also recorded that certain amounts payable to deceased workmen shall be paid to their legal heirs in accordance 
with law. 


The Tribunal has carefully perused the Memorandum of Settlement, the annexures thereto and the signatures of the 
parties. The settlement appears to be lawful, voluntary and arrived at during conciliation of the pending industrial 
dispute. There is nothing on record to suggest coercion, fraud, misrepresentation or undue influence. 


The law is well settled that a fair and bona fide settlement of an industrial dispute deserves acceptance by the 
adjudicating authority and should ordinarily be given effect to. 


In Herbertsons Ltd. v. Workmen of Herbertsons Ltd., (l977) 2 SCC 687 : 977 Lab IC 62], the Hon’ble Supreme 
Court held that a settlement arrived at between management and workmen in the course of collective bargaining 
should be respected and given due weight unless shown to be unfair, unjust or mala fide. 


Similarly, in National Engineering Industries Ltd. v. State of Rajasthan, (2000) | SCC 37] : 2000-I-LLJ-247 (SC), the 
Hon’ble Supreme Court reiterated that settlements entered into by parties under the Industrial Disputes Act constitute 
an important method of industrial peace and industrial adjudication should ordinarily encourage and uphold such 
settlements when they are fair and voluntary. 


Having examined the terms of settlement and the surrounding circumstances, this Tribunal is satisfied that the 
settlement is just, fair, reasonable and in the larger interest of industrial harmony. The settlement comprehensively 
resolves all disputes forming the subject matter of the present reference. 


Accordingly, the Reference deserves to be disposed of in terms of the settlement. 


AWARD 


The Memorandum of Settlement executed between Air India Limited and the 52 Claimants represented through 
Kamgar Utkarsha Sabha is hereby taken on record and shall form part of this Award. 


The Reference No. CGIT-/23 of 2003 is answered in terms of the said Settlement. 


It is declared that the rights and liabilities of the parties shall stand governed by the terms and conditions contained in 
the Memorandum of Settlement and Annexure-A appended thereto. 


Air India Limited shall make payment of the amounts specified in Annexure-A to the respective claimants and/or 
legal heirs, wherever applicable, in accordance with the terms of the Settlement. 


Upon such compliance, all claims, demands and disputes forming the subject matter of the present reference shall 
stand finally settled and extinguished. 


The Settlement shall be binding upon the parties in accordance with the provisions of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
947. 


Accordingly, the present Reference is disposed of and a No Dispute Award is passed in terms of the Settlement. 


Let a copy of this Award be sent to the Central Government for publication as required under Section |7 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947. 


Justice ARVIND KUMAR MISHRA, Presiding Officer

On .2.2025, the proxy counsel appearing on behalf of the claimant had sought further time to file an 
application for impleading the legal heirs in place of the deceased claimant. It was submitted that the widow of the 
deceased claimant had also unfortunately expired, and that only their children survived. 


Record perused. It is noted that on 4.0.2025, the proxy counsel appearing on behalf of the claimant had 
submitted that the claimant had expired and that an application for impleadment of legal heirs in place of the deceased 
claimant would be moved. 


As a tule, an application for impleadment of legal heirs ought to be filed within 90 days. However, more 
than one year has elapsed, but no such application has been filed till date. Record further reveals that the cross- 
examination of the claimant had been deferred on 0.04.2023. 


In these circumstances, when no application for impleadment of legal heirs has been filed for a 
considerable period and no one has appeared on behalf of the claimant to participate in the proceedings, this Tribunal 
has no other option but to pass a ‘No Dispute Award’ in the matter. Award is passed accordingly. Let a copy of this 
Award be sent to the Appropriate Government for publication/notification under Section 7 of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, |947. File be consigned to the Record Room.

Sh. Manoj Kumar and I] others Vs. The CEO, M/s CELEBI Delhi Cargo Terminal Management India Pvt. 
Ltd. and another. 


2. In the endorsement made in reference order, the appropriate Government commanded the parties raising the 
dispute to file statement of claim, complete with relevant documents, list of reliance and witnesses with this Tribunal 
within fifteen days of receipt of the reference order and to forward a copy of such statement of claim to the opposite 
parties involved in the dispute. Despite directions so given, Claimant opted not to file the claim statement with the 
Tribunal. 


3. On receipt of the above reference, notice was sent to the workman as well as the managements. In pursuance 
to the notices sent, none appeared on behalf of the claimant. No claim statement was filed on behalf of workman 
despite repeated adjournments. Thus, it is clear that the workman is not interested in adjudication of the reference on 
merits.

this award be sent to the appropriate Government, as required under Section !7 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947, 
for publication.

AWARD 


The Government of India, Ministry of Labour by its order No. L-2902//208-IR(M) dated 3.04.208 
referred the following dispute under section 0()(d) of the ID. Act, 947 for adjudication to this Tribunal between 
the management of M/s Andhra Cements Limited, and their workman. The reference is, 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether there has been termination of the services of Sri Ch. Demudu, S/o Late Sanyasi, supplied by M/s Aztec 
services(contractor) by the management of Visakha Cement Works units of Andhra Cements Limited/Jaypee Group 
of company (principal employer) in October 200 as alleged by Sri Demudu or Sri Ch. Demudu, S/o Late Sanyasi by 
M/s Aztec Services (contractor) has on their own suddenly stopped working on 0I.0.20l0 as alleged by the 
management of Visakha Cement Woks, Andhra Cements Limited/Jaypee Group of company (principal employer)? 


Whether the action of the management of Visakha Cement Works, Jaypee Group of company (present principal 
employer) in not considering Sri Ch. Demudu, S/o Late Sanyasi or services is fair, legal and justified? If not, to what 
relief the concerned workman is entitled and what directions (if any) are necessary in this regard? 


Whether the action of the present management of Visakha Cement Works in not paying benefits for the past services 
rendered to Visakha Cement Works by Sri Ch. Demudu, S/o Late Sanyasi is legal and justified? If not, to what relief 
the concerned workman is entitled and what directions are necessary in this regard?” 


The reference is numbered in this Tribunal as I.D. No 66/208 and notices were issued to the parties concerned. 


225 On the date fixed for appearance and filing of claim statement Petitioner remained absent. None appeared on 
behalf of the petitioner. Respondent is present. Notice sent to the petitioner was returned with the endorsement 
"Expired." No legal representative has come on record on behalf of the deceased petitioner despite service of notice. 
Accordingly, as there is no one to prosecute the case, a No Claim Award is passed. The reference is answered 
accordingly. 


Award is passed accordingly. Transmit.

AWARD 


The Government of India, Ministry of Labour by its order No. L-2902/24/20i8-IR(M) dated 6.04.208 
referred the following dispute under section 0()(d) of the ID. Act, 947 for adjudication to this Tribunal between 
the management of M/s Andhra Cements Limited, and their workman. The reference is, 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether there has been termination of the services of Sri Chandaka Simhachalam, S/o Durgalu, supplied by M/s 
Aztec services(contractor) by the management of Visakha Cement Works units of Andhra Cements Limited/Jaypee 
Group of company (principal employer) in October 200 as alleged by Sri Chandaka Simhachalam or Sri Chandaka 
Simhachalam, S/o Durgalu by M/s Aztec Services (contractor) has on their own suddenly stopped working on 
0I.0.20i0 as alleged by the management of Visakha Cement Woks, Andhra Cements Limited/Jaypee Group of 
company (principal employer)? 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : अगस्त 22, 2026/ श्रावण 3, 948 9503 


Whether the action of the management of Visakha Cement Works, Jaypee Group of company (present principal 
employer) in not considering Sri Chandaka Simhachalam, S/o Durgalu or services is fair, legal and justified? If not, to 
what relief the concerned workman is entitled and what directions (if any) are necessary in this regard? 


Whether the action of the present management of Visakha Cement Works in not paying benefits for the past services 
rendered to Visakha Cement Works by Sri Chandaka Simhachalam, S/o Durgalu is legal and justified? If not, to what 
relief the concerned workman is entitled and what directions are necessary in this regard?” 


The reference is numbered in this Tribunal as ID. No 78/20l8 and notices were issued to the parties 
concerned. 


2. On the date fixed for appearance and filing of claim statement Petitioner remained absent. Despite sufficient 
opportunity, Petitioner has failed to appear and did not file any claim statement. Hence, a No Claim Award is 
passed. The reference is answered accordingly. 


Award is passed accordingly. Transmit.

Government hereby publishes the award (Reference No. 38/203-4) of the Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal cum Labour Court, Nagpur as shown in the Annexure, in the Industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited and Sri Vinod Parate which was received along with soft 
copy of the award by the Central Government on !.08.2026. 


[No. L-3002/2/203- IR(M)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 
ANNEXURE 
Reserved On 4.06.2026 
Delivered on 5.07.2026 


CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, NAGPUR 
CASE NO. CGIT/NGP/38/203-4 


Vinod Parate— Petitioner 
Vs 
Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited - Management 


Present: Shri. Kavimandal for the Petitioner. 


N. W. Almelkar advocate for the Management. 


ik In exercise of powers conferred by clause D of sub section | and sub section 2-A of section l0 of the 
industrial disputes act, 947 (4 of 947) (the act in short). The central government has referred the industrial dispute 
between the employer in relation to the management of Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited and their workman 
Shri. Vinod Parate, HPCL workers organization in adjudication as per letter No. L-3002/I2/20l3 IR(M) dt. 
8.06.203 with the following schedule: -Whether the action of the disciplinary authority HPCL, Nagpur in 
imposing the penalty of dismissal from service on Shri. Vinod Parate, Chief Administrative Clerk vide his 
order dt. 08.08.202 is just fair and legal? If not to what relief the applicant entitled to? 


2. On receipt of the reference parties were noticed to file their respective statement of claim and written 
statement in response to which the workman in short file the statement of claim and the management of HPCL, Party 
No. | in short file their written statement. 


3. Workman file a statement of claim through the union by asserting that he was appointed in the HPCL as a 
clerk on 7.07.979 at Khapri LPG plant and he was promoted as General Clerk in June 992 and was posted at LPG 
department Nagpur, Regional Office, Sadar, Nagpur. He was working their till 998. In august 998 he was 
transferred to Khapri at Nagpur LPG Regional Office as Senior Assistant. He was working their till the period 2003. 
The plant of the Party No. | and the offices at Khapri are in the same premises. He was reassigned to Khapri LPG 
plant on lateral basis till 2005. In the year 2005 he was again transferred to Nagpur LPG Regional office. In the year 
2006 he was promoted as chief administrative assistant. In 2007 he was again transferred at Khapri LPG plant. He 
further submits that since the date of his initial joining till his suspension dt. 2.02.2008 he was working sincerely and 
honestly with Party No. |. From the progress of the Party No. 2, it can be seen that he was performing his duty and 


[भागवा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : अगस्त 22, 2026/ श्रावण 3, 948 957 


was thus on merits was granted permission. The service record of the Party No. 2 is clean excellent and unblemished. 
During entire service tenure of the Party No. 2, he was never served with any memo, warning or chargesheet. It is also 
contended that the Party No. 2 was union member. The officers of the Party No. | were not happy with the progress 
of the Party No. 2 and his union activity. Suddenly on 2.02.2008 Party No. 2 was suspended without his fault. While 
suspending the Party No. 2 it was alleged that he has committed grievous misconduct for preparing of fake BDS 
update option. Since his suspension from 2.02.2008 no action was taken against the Party No. 2 till the chargesheet 
dt. .09.2009 was issued to him. a chargesheet was issued against the Party No. 2 after almost | year and 3 months 
which is prohibited under the standing orders applicable to the Party No. 2 establishment. As per the standing order 
clause 32(4)(A) statement of charges in detail has to be supplied within 7 days of suspension however in the case of 
Party No. 2 the chargesheet was issued after !5 months of suspension information office. On .05.2009 he was 
suspended and on 42.02.2008 a chargesheet was issued. At the time of suspension, no documents or statements were 
supplied to the Party No. 2. The chargesheet dt. !.05.2009 was issued along with annexure I, I, III and IV. The 
charges level in annexure I article of charge ॥* A was in respect of preparation of cash receipt and manual fitting the 
BDS nos. by using the BDS update option intentionally. Charge B was in respect of fraudulently preparing cash 
receipt in the JDE system using Shri.V. G. Paunikers transactions ID in the name of Jeevan Gas agency and 
Satyashree Gas company with fictitious cheque no. and in order to enable the dealers to unlawfully gain for 
themselves thereby causing financial loss by making false entries by using BDS update option. Chargesheet was in 
respect of deliberately misusing ERP excess of Shri. V.G. Paunikar for preparing erroneous BDS nos. by updating 
BDS update option causing financial loss to the corporation. Charge D was in respect of neglecting while preparing 
in the CR in respect Siva Gas Agency. Charge E was in respect of deliberately concealing all the transaction 
concerning movable property including loans and advances. The said misconduct as alleged is covered under the 
clauses 6A, 3I(4), 3I(5), 3(9), 3€C5), 3(20), 3I(38) and 3(39) of the standing orders applicable to the non- 
management employees working in the marketing division of the corporation. It is contended by Party No. 2 that the 
charges levelled against the Party No. 2 was false concocted and levelled without having any material evidence along 
with the chargesheet dt. !.05.2009 no relevant documents were supplied to the Party No. 2 in respect of the charges 
levelled. The Party No. 2 demanded documents vide letter dt. l8.05.2009 from the Party No. . The management 
Party No. | replied by letter dt. !9.05.2009 and supplied certain documents which were not at all according to the 
demand made by the Party No. 2. However, the Party No. 2 without wasting any further time replied to the 
chargesheet vide reply dt. 28.05.2009. thereafter the intimation dt. !.05.2009 was given to the Party No. 2 regarding 
appointment of enquiry officer. 


4. The departmental inquiry was conducted against the Party No.2 which was not according to the principals of 
natural justice and was continued for a period of more than three months. The Party No. 2 was dismissed from 
services on basis of the said enquiry by an order dt. 08/08/202. Party ९०. management examined total 5 witnesses. 
The main charges levelled against the Party No.2 was for the period of 2005 to 2007. It was alleged in the charges that 
the Party No. 2 on 8/08/2006 and !6/09/2006 cash receipts were made in favour of Jeevan Gas Agency from Shri. 
V.G. Paunikar’s I.D.by the Party No.2 from his computer, though Shri V.G Paunikar was not posted during the said 
tenure in Khapri LPG Plant. It was further observed that on 08.08.2006, C.R. was prepared in the name of Jeevan Gas 
Agency from D.D No.86599, though no such instrument was ever issued by the bank. Similarly, on 6.09.2006 C.R. 
was prepared in the name of Jeevan Gas Agency from D.D No.8772, though the bank had denied issuance of such 
D.D in favour of HPCL. Further it was alleged that similar fraudulent entries were made from Shri.V.G Paunikar’s 
I.D. though later was not handling the said activities. The memorandum of charges is described in the chargesheet dt.

5. It is also false and denied that the Enquiry conducted against the Party No. 2 workman was against the

specific bar for him to engage an outsider to defend himself, even if his representative was a lawyer by profession. It

be supplied within 7 days of suspension order but in the present case charge sheet was issued to the Party No. 2 by the 
Party No. | after 5 months of suspension order. 


22. It is also contended that chargesheet dt. .05.2009 was issued to the Party No. 2 along with annexure I-IV.

receipts in the JDE system using transactions ID Shri.V. G. Paunikers in the name of Jeevan Gas agency and 
Satyashree Gas company with fictitious cheque nos. in order to enable the dealers to unlawfully gain for themselves 
delivery of LPG cylinders without paying money to Party No. | thereby causing financial loss to the corporation by 
making false entries by using BDS update option. Charge C was in respect of deliberately using ERP excess of Shri. 
V.G. Paunikar for preparing erroneous BDS nos. causing financial loss to the corporation. Charge D was in respect of 
neglecting while preparing in the CR in respect Siva Gas Agency. Charge E was in respect of deliberately concealing 
all the transaction concerning movable property including loans and advances. It has been argued on behalf of the 
respondent that the aforesaid act on the part of the workman constituted misconduct under clauses 6A, 3(4), 3(5), 
3(9), 3€5), 3(20), 3(38) and 3(39) of the certified standing orders applicable to the workman.

in the name of HPCL after depositing the same in the office of HPCL he used to collect loads of filed gas cylinders 
from Khapri LPG Plant. The MW-I pointed out that he was not highly educated and had studied up to 27 standard

25. Through this letter, Shri Junghare had admitted that he fell into a trap when the Workman Shri Vinod Parate, 
the then Employee of HPCL, had lured him to commit a fraud and grab money by cheating HPCL. On that occasion, 
the Workman had asked Shri Junghare to deposit the Bank Demand Draft of Rs. 86,000/- in HPCL to lift the load of 
gas cylinders. A Cash Receipt Number 7005044 dt. 25.06.2007 was prepared and issued by workman Shri Parate in 
the said transaction in favour of the Dealer (Mr. Jeevan Gas Agency) for SBI Demand Draft Number 934] dt. 
25.06.2007. Shri Junghare was then supposed to lift the load of gas cylinders. However, the Workman Shri Parate had 
promised Shri. Junghare to return the Bank Demand Draft to him with an understanding that Shri Junghare cancel the 
Demand Draft and collect the amount against it and then said amount would be shared equally by both of them, ie., 
Shri Junghare and Workman Shri Parate. Shri Junghare also admitted that he had accepted the aforesaid offer given to 
him by workman Shri Parate as he (Shri Junghare) was facing financial problems. Out of greed, to grab money, he 
had fallen into the aforesaid trap of workman Shri. Parate. 


26. MW-| has categorically deposed that accordingly, Shri Junghare had purchased the Demand Draft of Rs. 
86,000/- from State Bank of India, Saoner Branch. It was deposited by him in HPCL. Load of gas cylinders was lifted 
against the said Demand Draft. Thereafter, the workman returned the Demand Draft to Shri Junghare, who in turn, 
deposited the said Demand Draft in the Bank, got it cancelled and against the cancellation of the Demand Draft, he 
got issued a Cheque from the Bank in his personal/ own Name. The MW-| further confirmed in his examination that

the cheque was deposited and encashed by Shri Junghare at a later stage and the amount of Rs. 86,000/- was equally 
shared (Rs. 43,000/- each) by Shri Junghare and workman Shri Parate subsequently. The MW-! also pointed out that 
Shri Junghare had admitted his guilt in his letter dt. 23.0.2008 which is at Exhibit- M-24. Incident was later reported 
to him by HPCL and at that stage, he gathered that Shri Manoj Junghare, his the then Manager, had committed a 
fraud, in collusion with Workman Shri Parate. It was clearly confirmed out by the MW-I that after the aforesaid 
episode, workman Shri Parate himself had contacted the ॥५७४-] on phone with an assurance that whatever amount he 
had received through a deal with Shri Manoj Junghare would be returned by him to the MW-|. In is every vital and

Shri. Junghare and the workman. 


27. The MW-| stated that there were similar instances in the year 2005-2006 also when HPCL had informed him 
that although no Demand Drafts were issued by the Bank in favour of HPCL for lifting the load of gas cylinders, Cash 
Receipts were issued in the name of his Gas Agency which had ultimately caused pecuniary loss to HPCL. The MW- 
| also pointed out that since he was totally unaware about these illegal transactions in the initial stage, he had lodged a 
Police Complaint with the Station Officer, Police Station, Khapa on 26.03.2008. Attention of this Hon’ble Tribunal is 
also drawn to the Police Complaint lodged by the MW-I! with Police Station, Khapa on 26.03.2008 which was filed 
on record at Serial No. l0 along with the List of Documents dt 09.04.20l4. The contents of the said Police 
Complaints support and corroborate what the MW-I has deposed in his evidence before this Hon’ble Tribunal. 
During the Cross-examination the MW-I, reiterated his deposition what he alleged in the examination-in-chief 
further, when a question was put to the witness by this Court, the ॥५७-। maintained as it is and stated that Manoj 
Junghare had committed a fraud against HPCL as well as M/s. Jeevan Gas Agency. From the aforesaid evidence and 
particularly the Cross-examination of the MW-l], it could be seen that the Witness has maintained and has stood by 
what he as deposed in his affidavit/examination in chief and there is absolutely nothing in his cross examination to 
disbelieve his testimony. It may also be kept in mind here that Shri Junghare has committed fraud at the instance of 
workman as Shri Parate had lured him and Shri Junghare had fallen into the trap laid down by workman Shri Parate in 
as much as Shri Junghare was suffering from financial crisis and wanted to make quick money. Shri Jeevan Singh has 
lodged a Police Complaint against Shri. Junghare and his other companion who jointly has committed fraud in a 
normal course no person would lodge a false police complaint. 


28. The testimony of Shri Jeevan Singh is consistent, trustworthy and in tune with the documents placed on 
record, particularly Exhibit M-22, Exhibit M-24 and also the Police Complaint lodged by him with Police Station, 
Khapa. The defence has not been able to falsify or discredit the testimony of this particular witness. Not only this 
applicant/petitioner has never denied the contents written in Exhibit M-22 and M-24. Jeevan Singh has admitted in his 
letter dt. 20.03.2008 Exhibit M-22 that the fraud has been committed by his manager Manoj Junghare in connivance 
with the officer of the HPCL and the letter which is written by Manoj Junghare on 23.0!.2008 addressed to the 
HPCL. The commitment of fraud has also been admitted by the Manoj Junghare with the connivance of Shri Vinod 
Parate. Learned counsel for the petitioner/ applicant has never denied in whole of the Statement of Claim. Vinod 
Parate has also been examined in his defence as ?५४-] but in his chief as well as in his cross-examination he never 
denied the averments written in the letter dt. 23.0!.2008 Exhibit M-24 and letter dt. 20.03.2008 Exhibit M-22. The 
applicant/ petitioner and his counsel if not denied it means the contents written in those letters are impliedly admitted 
to the applicant. 


29. The Workman Shri Rakesh Kumar Vaimanya MW-72 has also been examined on behalf of the management 
to prove the contentions written in the Written Statement. The MW-2 was working as Dy. Manager Finance at Khapri 
LPG Plant during the relevant period when the Workman was also employed at the said Khapri LPG Plant. The MW- 
2 was posted at Mumbai prior to his transfer to Khapri LPG Plant and he was well conversant with the computerised 
system which was introduced at various locations of HPCL throughout India, including Khapri LPG Plant, to keep the 
computerised record of accounting and financial transactions. This witness has noticed various discrepancies and 
irregularities in the financial transactions in the matter of supply of LPG Gas Cylinders to the Dealers of HPCL, 
without actually receiving the amount in the Bank of HPCL against the Cash Receipts. The MW-2 explained that cash 
Receipts were prepared by the concerned staff in the office of HPCL at its Khapri LPG Plant, but actually, the 
instrument i.e., Demand Drafts/ Cheques were not deposited in the Bank though Bank Deposit Slips (BDS). It was 
later revealed during the close scrutiny that those instruments were never deposited in the Bank Account of HPCL 
through BDS with the result that it caused huge financial loss to HPCL. In fact, according to the MW-2 although the 
instrument for cash receipts were never deposited in the Bank, loads (filled gas cylinders) were released to the dealers. 
HPCL did not receive money/ consideration from its Dealers and yet products/ loads were delivered to them. The 
MW-2? pointed out that the consideration amount is mandatorily and compulsorily accepted by HPCL from its Dealers 
through Banker’s Demand Drafts/ Cheques which are negotiable instruments. However, the words “Cash Receipt” 
were used in common parlance in the internal business transactions of HPCL against the Demand Drafts/ Cheques 
received from the Dealers. 


30. He further stated that the system generates BDS automatically on giving BDS print command, basis the 
details available in the cash receipt. He further mentioned that the user normally gives certain inputs in the system 


9526 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : AUGUST 22, 2026/ SHRAVAN 3], 948 [Part II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


through BDS print version and other details are automatically captured by the system from the CRs. The MW-2 
further clarified that there is no difference in generation of BDS for post-dated cheques since the system generates 
BDS as per the cash receipt due dates only. As to specific query of the PO, the MW-2 admitted that BDS number can 
be manually updated in the JDE since such provision is available in the system under cash receipt menu/ program as 
“BDS update”. The MW-2 further mentioned that BDS update Option has been provided in JDE to rectify any error 
or in a cash receipt for which the BDS in final mode had already been taken. In normal circumstances, one is required 
to cancel the incorrect CR which is made with wrong inputs and prepare fresh CRs with correct inputs and manually 
update the same with concerned BDS. As to specific query of the PO. The witness stated that wrong inputs meant 
inputs such as incorrect customer code, wrong amount, erroneous dates, etc. However, the person using BDS update 
option is required to ensure cancellation of the cash receipts made with wrong inputs and make fresh cash receipts and 
update the same with the concerned BDS. This witness has also stated that when financial irregularities were noticed 
by him and when it was found that Cash Receipts were prepared but instruments were never deposited in the Bank 
through BDS, and yet, the load of Gas Cylinders was released in favour of the concerned Dealers of HPCL, he 
personally took up the matter with the concerned Dealers and took them into confidence. The MW-2 reconciled their 
Accounts and when the Dealers were also satisfied that they had lifted the load without depositing the Demand Drafts/ 
Cheques in the office of HPCL, the MW-2 was successful in recovering substantial outstanding amounts from such 
dealers. This witness also named various Gas Agencies in relation to whom financial irregularities were noticed and 
they included M/s Prayagraj Gas Agency, M/s. Jeevan Gas Agency, M/s. Pendam Gas Agency, M/s. Satyashree Gas 
Company, etc. it was also revealed by the bankers of these dealers that in certain cases the Demand Draft were not 
issued in favour of the HPCL at all by these respective banks. Although cash receipts entries were made in the 
computerised system of HPCL at its Khapri Plant. MW-2 referred and deposed the same incident about which the 
Proprietor of M/s. Jeevan Gas Agency, Shri Jeevan Singh Wayam (MW-]) had deposed in detail earlier. Since that 
part of the deposition of Shri Rakesh Kumar is the corroboration of what is deposed by the MW-l. The MW-2 also 
pointed out that the manager of M/s. Jeevan Gas Agency, Shri Junghare had colluded with the Workman, Shri Vinod 
Parate, and had defrauded HPCL and had shared the amount involved, between two of them. This witness has also 
stated that the proprietor of M/s Jeevan Gas Agency had written a letter dt. 6.04.2008 to the Vigilance Officer of 
HPCL. It is as Exhibit M-28. In that letter, the Proprietor had stated that Demand Draft No. 934I] was cancelled by 
his manager Shri. Manoj Jhunghare without his knowledge and that the amount was siphoned off from his account. 
The MW-2 has referred to Exhibit- M-24, a letter dt. 23.0.2008 in which the Manager, Shri Manoj Junghare had also 
admitted that he had committed a fraud in collusion with Workman Shri Parate and that the money was shared by 
both of them. This position was confirmed by the MW-| in his aforesaid letter Exhibit - M-28. It could also be seen 
that in the said letter Exhibit M-28, the ॥४७४७-] has empathetically stated that on one occasion, workman Shri Parate 
had called/ phoned him saying that he (Workman Shri Parate) would refund the money if Jeevan Singh was ready to 
withdraw his letter addressed to HPCL. The MW- 2 has then referred to Exhibit- M-29 which is a detailed Statement 
prepared by him with regard to recoveries made from various Dealers. It pertains to such cases where Cash Receipts 
were made but instruments were not deposited in the Bank of HPCL through BDS. However, due to the efforts made 
by the MW-2 substantial recovery was made from the Dealers and that is also reflected in Exhibit- M-29. The MW-2 
has further stated that Exhibit M-30 is the extract of JDE Statement reflecting the details of Accounts showing 
instruments. These are the Screenshots taken from the computer. After referring to the details in Exhibit M-30, the 
MW-2 has pointed out that both these batches were prepared form employee used ID 30070320 which is the 
employee ID of the workman. This updation was done manually with a malafide intention, instead of opting for 
Automatic System Updating process. The MW-2 has deposed further that State Bank of India, Saoner Branch had 
addressed one letter dt. !2.04.2008 to M/s. Jeevan Gas Agency, Khapa. It has been filed on record by the Party No. | 
(HPCL) before this Hon’ble Tribunal, at Serial No. 2] along with the list of documents dt. 09.04.204. The Bank had 
informed inter-alia that although certain Demand Drafts were issued by the Branch in the name of HPCL, there were 
as many as five other Demand Drafts for various amounts which were not issued by the Bank in favour of HPCL. The 
MW-2 has emphasized that from this letter of bank, it was clear that certain Demand Drafts were not brought to the 
Office of HPCL, Khapri LPG Plant and yet Cash Receipts were generated against those Demand Drafts, although 
they were not issued in favour of HPCL. Thus, false instruments were generated through computerised system. The 
MW-2 concluded his deposition by stating that this particular letter dt. 2.04.2008 issued by State Bank of India, 
Saoner Branch, supported his findings that a fraud was committed against HPCL and loads of gas cylinders were 
lifted without depositing the Demand Drafts with HPCL as consideration, thereby causing huge financial loss to 
HPCL. The said letter of State Bank of India is at Exhibit M-32. The MW-2 during his cross examination confronted 
that the workman was working at LPG Regional Office from June 2006 to May 2007 and that he later on worked at 
Khapri LPG Plant from 09.06.2007. However, it is pertinent to note here Nagpur LPG Plant are both situated in the 
same Office Building within the premises of Khapri LPG Bottling Plant. Therefore, the workman cannot take 
advantage of his place of work during these two periods in as much as he was working within the premises of Khapri 
LPG Plant on both the occasions. The workman Shri Parate had taken advantage of manual updation facility available 
in the computerised system to siphond off money which actually belonged to HPCL by adopting the modous operandi 
of making entries of negotiable instruments, Demand Drafts, cheques in the computerised system and along with 
dealers to lift the loads of gas cylinders although such negotiable instruments were actually never deposited in the 


[भागवा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : अगस्त 22, 2026/ श्रावण 3, 948 9527 


bank account of HPCL. Again, the document speak for themselves and these documents cannot be brushed under the 
carpet as these documents and the deposition of these two witnesses are in tandem and support each other. 


3s Shri Madhukar Igole has been examined as MW-3 in support of the contentions of the management. He has 
also supported the contentions as alleged by the management against the delinquent workman Shri. Vinod Parate. He 
has been cross-examined by learned counsel for the respondent but this witness has consistently supported the version 
of the management which is alleged against the delinquent employee. 


32. The next and the last witness examined by HPCL in the case is Shri Kamlesh Sheth who at the relevant point

particular incident involving one Shri V. G. Paunikar. Shri Paunikar had submitted a leave application to the

highlighted the fact that a Cash Receipt was generated using Shri Paunikar’s ID. Although he was on leave on that 
particular day. In the event an employee was on leave at that point of time, he could not prepare a cash receipt for any 
customer since at the relevant time JDE access was available only in the corporation’s office which is also evident 
from the deposition of workman during his cross examination. Exhibit M-26 provides login details given by HQO 
ERP Server (software. As per the above login report, the login ID of two employees i.e., Employee Number 3070320 
(Vinod A. Parate) and Employee Number 3006!250 (V. G. Paunikar) were used to log-in to JDE Software system 
through same IP Address on 08.08.2006, 6.09.2006, 25.06.2007 and 4.07.2007. Shri V. G. Paunikar was assigned

LPG Plant premises wherein Nagpur LPG RO is also situated. The MW-4 has then referred to the Statement given by 
the workman on 20.03.2008 in the presence of the then Deputy Chief Vigilance Officer. It is at Exhibit M-38 and the 
original copy has also been filed on record subsequently. The workman has signed the said Statement on every page. 
Thus, the workman confirmed, by putting his signatures on all the six pages, that his Statement was correctly 
recorded. In that Statement Exhibit M-38, the workman admitted that he was physically present at the Nagpur LPG 
Regional Office on 08.08.2006. During the recording of his Statement, the workman was shown the attendance 
register for the said date and, upon verification, confirmed that the signature appearing therein was his own. This 
admission on the part of the workman clearly indicates that although Shri V.G. Paunikar was working in Nagpur City 
(LIC Building) at that time, workman Shri Parate had used his Log-in-ID while sitting at Khapri LPG Plant Premises. 


33. Not only this, the workman has also admitted that his duties also included cheque preparation, Preparation of 
Cash Receipts, printing of Bank Deposit Slip (BDS), etc. Hence, these admissions of the workman clearly fortify the 
stand of HPCL that the duties assigned to him included preparation of BDS, Cash Receipts, etc. Hence these

BDS, Cash Receipts, etc. Shri Manoj Junghare vide his letter dt. 23.0!.2008 & 3.02.2008 at Exhibit M-24 & Exhibit 
M-0, clearly gives an inference that workman Shri Parate in connivance with Shri Manoj Junghare had misused the 
ERP access of Shri V.G. Paunikar and committed the fraudulent activities by using the BDS Update Option thereby 
giving credit to the respective dealers/ customers and assisting them to lift the product basis the same at the expense 
of the corporation. While referring to another document at Exhibit M-30 which is the screen shot taken from the 
computer about the extract of JDE Statements, witness has highlighted that in this particular document, remarks 
column was left blank deliberately so that further manipulation could be done while preparing Cash Receipts and 
Bank Deposit Slips using manual updation option, generally in the remark’s column, BDS details will be reflected. 
However, since the workman updated the entries using manual updation option, he kept the column blank 
deliberately. 


34. MW-4 has then referred to other set of JDE screenshots taken from the computer system and placed on 
record along with the list of documents dt. 08.04.2025. They pertain to Cash Receipts Nos. 6007343, 600970I and 
7005044, all created in favour of M/s. Jeevan Gas Agency and corresponding Demand Draft Nos. are 86599, 87!72

86599 and 8772 were not issued by the Bank at all, whereas Cheque No. 934]] was issued by the Bank in favour of 
HPCL. The discrepancies in the record, therefore, speak volumes about the Mal-practices adopted by the workman 
using not only his own log-in ID but also that of Shri Paunikar. 


35. All the four witnesses adduced by the management in support of his contention have deposed consistently 
and in complete corroboration with each other not only through their oral testimonies but also by specifically referring 
to the documents already placed on record and duly exhibited before this court. Accordingly, the depositions of all 
four witnesses are mutually supportive, consistent, and reliable, particularly when nothing material has emerged

37. Insofar as the alleged delay in issuance of the charge-sheet to the workman is concerned, the corporation has 
already explained, in detail, the reasons for such delay in its Reply. The management was required to undertake 
careful scrutiny of voluminous transactions, computerized data, and related records, which necessarily involved a 
detailed preliminary investigation before issuance of the charge-sheet. Therefore, chargesheet was filed against the 
delinquent employee after careful scrutiny of the matter. Therefore, the workman has completely failed to establish 
his contention that false charges were levelled against him. in any case it was incumbent upon the workman to 
specifically plead and prove that HPCL and its officials were actuated by mala fide intentions or ulterior motives to 
falsely implicate him for any particular reason. The workman has utterly failed to establish any such ill motive, bias, 
personal animosity, or ulterior purpose on the part of the Management or its officials. The workman Shri Vinod Parate 
has admitted that the JD Edwards Enterprises One (JDE) Software System was introduced in HPCL in the year 2004 
and that he was fully aware of the said system. The workman has also admitted that the said software system could be 
accessed only from within the office premises of HPCL and not from any external location. Significantly, he has 
further admitted that if any employee was on leave, it was not possible for such employee to access the said software 
system. These admissions clearly and unequivocally establish that the workman had logged into the JOE System by 
unauthorizedly and illegally using the Login ID of Shri Paunikar, his colleague, during the period when Shri Paunikar 
was on leave. No other person was suggested to perform this work at the relevant point of time in the work place 
mentioned above. 


38. Mere denial of the charges or to say that he was not entrusted the work of handling software, preparing BDS, 
depositing negotiable instruments in the bank account of the HPCL cannot be accepted at all without any concrete 
proof in support thereof. 


39. It is also argued on behalf of the management that management has completely lost confidence in the 
workman. Therefore, the acts involving financial irregularities, misappropriation or breach of trust are viewed with 
utmost seriousness. In the instant case the conduct of the workman as established on record has caused serious 
financial prejudice to the establishment and has irreparably shaken the confidence reposed in him by the management. 
In such circumstances the continuance of the workman in service was neither desirable nor in the interest of the 
organization. Accordingly having bonafide lost confidence in the workman, the management took the decision to 
dismiss him from service which action is fully justified, legal, and proper in the facts and circumstances of the present 
case. 


40. Although, learned counsel for the applicant/ petitioner has argued that Jeevan Singh is hearsay witness. He 
has not seen the occurrence before his eyes. Therefore, the evidentiary value of the hearsay witness is not credible.

Whereas this letter was addressed to Senior Manager Nagpur Khapri Branch. It transpires that this letter was prepared 
by Vinod Parate with connivance of Mr. Manoj Jhunghare. But the applicant Shri Vinod Parate has never adduced 
Manoj Jhunghare to prove the averments written in this letter. Therefore, the contents of this letter are not proved. 
Therefore, it has no value in evidence.
